T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     lstrip(str::AbstractString, chars)::SubString
T 49 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Remove leading characters from `str`, either those specified by `chars` or those for
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L which the function `pred` returns `true`.
T 41 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The default behaviour is to remove leading whitespace and delimiters: see
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L [`isspace`](@ref) for precise details.
T 38 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The optional `chars` argument specifies which characters to remove: it can be a single
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L character, or a vector or set of characters.
T 44 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`strip`](@ref), [`rstrip`](@ref).
T 43 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     rpad(s, n::Integer, p::Union{AbstractChar,AbstractString}=' ')::String
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Stringify `s` and pad the resulting string on the right with `p` to make it `n`
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L characters (in [`textwidth`](@ref)) long. If `s` is already `n` characters long, an equal
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L string is returned. Pad with spaces by default.
T 47 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.7"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     In Julia 1.7, this function was changed to use `textwidth` rather than a raw character (codepoint) count.
T 109 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`lpad`](@ref).
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> rpad("March", 20)
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "March               "
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     eachsplit(str::AbstractString, dlm; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=true)
T 79 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     eachsplit(str::AbstractString; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=false)
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Split `str` on occurrences of the delimiter(s) `dlm` and return an iterator over the
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L substrings.  `dlm` can be any of the formats allowed by [`findnext`](@ref)'s first argument
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (i.e. as a string, regular expression or a function), or as a single character or collection
T 92 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L of characters.
T 14 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If `dlm` is omitted, it defaults to [`isspace`](@ref).
T 54 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The optional keyword arguments are:
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - `limit`: the maximum size of the result. `limit=0` implies no maximum (default)
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - `keepempty`: whether empty fields should be kept in the result. Default is `false` without
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    a `dlm` argument, `true` with a `dlm` argument.
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.8"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     The `eachsplit` function requires at least Julia 1.8.
T 57 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`split`](@ref).
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = "Ma.rch"
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "Ma.rch"
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> b = eachsplit(a, ".")
T 28 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L Base.SplitIterator{String, String}("Ma.rch", ".", 0, true)
T 58 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> collect(b)
T 17 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "Ma"
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "rch"
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """

L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     eachrsplit(str::AbstractString, dlm; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=true)
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     eachrsplit(str::AbstractString; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=false)
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Return an iterator over `SubString`s of `str`, produced when splitting on
T 73 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the delimiter(s) `dlm`, and yielded in reverse order (from right to left).
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `dlm` can be any of the formats allowed by [`findprev`](@ref)'s first argument
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (i.e. a string, a single character or a function), or a collection of characters.
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If `dlm` is omitted, it defaults to [`isspace`](@ref), and `keepempty` default to `false`.
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The optional keyword arguments are:
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - If `limit > 0`, the iterator will split at most `limit - 1` times before returning
T 85 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    the rest of the string unsplit. `limit < 1` implies no cap to splits (default).
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - `keepempty`: whether empty fields should be returned when iterating
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    Default is `false` without a `dlm` argument, `true` with a `dlm` argument.
T 77 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Note that unlike [`split`](@ref), [`rsplit`](@ref) and [`eachsplit`](@ref), this
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L function iterates the substrings right to left as they occur in the input.
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.11"
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     This function requires Julia 1.11 or later.
T 47 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`eachsplit`](@ref), [`rsplit`](@ref).
T 47 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = "Ma.r.ch";
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> collect(eachrsplit(a, ".")) == ["ch", "r", "Ma"]
T 55 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> collect(eachrsplit(a, "."; limit=2)) == ["ch", "Ma.r"]
T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L true
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     split(str::AbstractString, dlm; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=true)
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     split(str::AbstractString; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=false)
T 71 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Split `str` into an array of substrings on occurrences of the delimiter(s) `dlm`.  `dlm`
T 88 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L can be any of the formats allowed by [`findnext`](@ref)'s first argument (i.e. as a
T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L string, regular expression or a function), or as a single character or collection of
T 84 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L characters.
T 11 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L If `dlm` is omitted, it defaults to [`isspace`](@ref).
T 54 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The optional keyword arguments are:
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - `limit`: the maximum size of the result. `limit=0` implies no maximum (default)
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  - `keepempty`: whether empty fields should be kept in the result. Default is `false` without
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L    a `dlm` argument, `true` with a `dlm` argument.
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`rsplit`](@ref), [`eachsplit`](@ref).
T 47 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = "Ma.rch"
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "Ma.rch"
T 8 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> split(a, ".")
T 20 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "Ma"
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "rch"
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     rsplit(s::AbstractString; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=false)
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     rsplit(s::AbstractString, chars; limit::Integer=0, keepempty::Bool=true)
T 76 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Similar to [`split`](@ref), but starting from the end of the string. The splits are found
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L from right to left (which matters e.g. when `limit` is given), but the substrings are still
T 91 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L returned in the order in which they appear in `s`.
T 50 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`eachrsplit`](@ref), which yields the substrings from right to left.
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = "M.a.r.c.h"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "M.a.r.c.h"
T 11 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> rsplit(a, ".")
T 21 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 5-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "M"
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "a"
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "r"
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "c"
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "h"
T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> rsplit(a, "."; limit=1)
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 1-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "M.a.r.c.h"
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> rsplit(a, "."; limit=2)
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2-element Vector{SubString{String}}:
T 36 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  "M.a.r.c"
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia

T 4 source.julia string.docstring.julia

L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     replace([io::IO], s::AbstractString, pat=>r, [pat2=>r2, ...]; [count::Integer])
T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Search for the given pattern `pat` in `s`, and replace each occurrence with `r`.
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L If `count` is provided, replace at most `count` occurrences.
T 60 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `pat` may be a single character, a vector or a set of characters, a string,
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L or a regular expression.
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L If `r` is a function, each occurrence is replaced with `r(s)`
T 61 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L where `s` is the matched substring (when `pat` is a `AbstractPattern` or `AbstractString`) or
T 93 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L character (when `pat` is an `AbstractChar` or a collection of `AbstractChar`).
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L If `pat` is a regular expression and `r` is a [`SubstitutionString`](@ref), then capture group
T 94 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L references in `r` are replaced with the corresponding matched text.
T 67 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L To remove instances of `pat` from `string`, set `r` to the empty `String` (`""`).
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The return value is a new string after the replacements.  If the `io::IO` argument
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L is supplied, the transformed string is instead written to `io` (returning `io`).
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L (For example, this can be used in conjunction with an [`IOBuffer`](@ref) to re-use
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L a pre-allocated buffer array in-place.)
T 39 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Multiple patterns can be specified: The input string will be scanned only once
T 78 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L from start (left) to end (right), and the first matching replacement
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L will be applied to each substring. Replacements are applied in the order of
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L the arguments provided if they match substrings starting at the same
T 68 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L input string position. Thus, only one pattern will be applied to any character, and the
T 87 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L patterns will only be applied to the input text, not the replacements.
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.7"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Support for multiple patterns requires version 1.7.
T 55 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.10"
T 23 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     The `io::IO` argument requires version 1.10.
T 48 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> replace("Python is a programming language.", "Python" => "Julia")
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "Julia is a programming language."
T 34 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> replace("The quick foxes run quickly.", "quick" => "slow", count=1)
T 74 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "The slow foxes run quickly."
T 29 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> replace("The quick foxes run quickly.", "quick" => "", count=1)
T 70 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "The  foxes run quickly."
T 25 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> replace("The quick foxes run quickly.", r"fox(es)?" => s"bus\\1")
T 67 source.julia string.docstring.julia
T 69 source.julia string.docstring.julia constant.character.escape.julia
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "The quick buses run quickly."
T 30 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> replace("abcabc", "a" => "b", "b" => "c", r".+" => "a")
T 62 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "bca"
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.end.julia

L # hex <-> bytes conversion
T 1 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia punctuation.definition.comment.julia
T 26 source.julia comment.line.number-sign.julia
L 
L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     hex2bytes(itr)
T 18 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Given an iterable `itr` of ASCII codes for a sequence of hexadecimal digits, returns a
T 86 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L `Vector{UInt8}` of bytes  corresponding to the binary representation: each successive pair
T 90 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L of hexadecimal digits in `itr` gives the value of one byte in the return vector.
T 80 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L The length of `itr` must be even, and the returned array has half of the length of `itr`.
T 89 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L See also [`hex2bytes!`](@ref) for an in-place version, and [`bytes2hex`](@ref) for the inverse.
T 95 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.7"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Calling `hex2bytes` with iterators producing `UInt8` values requires
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Julia 1.7 or later. In earlier versions, you can `collect` the iterator
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     before calling `hex2bytes`.
T 31 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> s = string(12345, base = 16)
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "3039"
T 6 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> hex2bytes(s)
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 2-element Vector{UInt8}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x30
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x39
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> a = b"01abEF"
T 20 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 6-element Base.CodeUnits{UInt8, String}:
T 40 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x30
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x31
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x61
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x62
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x45
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x46
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L julia> hex2bytes(a)
T 19 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 3-element Vector{UInt8}:
T 24 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0x01
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0xab
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L  0xef
T 5 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L """

L """
T 3 source.julia string.docstring.julia punctuation.definition.string.begin.julia
L     bytes2hex(itr)::String
T 26 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     bytes2hex(io::IO, itr)::Nothing
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L Convert an iterator `itr` of bytes to its hexadecimal string representation, either
T 83 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L returning a `String` via `bytes2hex(itr)` or writing the string to an `io` stream
T 81 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L via `bytes2hex(io, itr)`.  The hexadecimal characters are all lowercase.
T 72 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L !!! compat "Julia 1.7"
T 22 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Calling `bytes2hex` with arbitrary iterators producing `UInt8` values requires
T 82 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     Julia 1.7 or later. In earlier versions, you can `collect` the iterator
T 75 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L     before calling `bytes2hex`.
T 31 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L See also [`hex2bytes`](@ref), [`hex2bytes!`](@ref).
T 51 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L 
L # Examples
T 10 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L ```jldoctest
T 12 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L julia> a = string(12345, base = 16)
T 35 source.julia string.docstring.julia
L "3039"
